Um... no, not really. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, my weight has stayed pretty much exactly the same, give or take a pound or two. My clothes still fit the way they always have since I've been home with my back.

Is that... does that sound like a lot to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight even when you're at a weight that other people consider normal?\n\nPatient: Um... well, people do tell me I look fine, and I really try to listen to them, I do. I don't want to seem vain or anything. But... honestly? Yes. I worry about it a lot. \n\nIt's just... even if the bathroom scale goes up by just one single pound, I get this awful panic. It makes me feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my back is just... turning me soft, you know? And I'll just start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. \n\nI know it sounds so silly to get worked up over one pound. I really should know better at my age, and I am trying to be more reasonable about it. Does... does that sound totally crazy to you? I hope you don't think I'm just being shallow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or types of food you eat to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... actually, no, I don't really do that. I hope that's okay to say. I mean, I try to just eat regular meals during the day, you know? I don't really cut out certain foods or put myself on strict diets or anything like that. \n\nI know a lot of women do that to stay in shape, and maybe I should have better willpower about my meals, but... I just try to eat normal things. I've never wanted to be one of those difficult people who can't eat anything when they go out or who makes a big fuss over a menu. I really do try to trust what others see. I hope that makes sense? I don't want to sound like I'm completely ignoring reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, my weight has stayed pretty much exactly the same, give or take a pound or two. My clothes still fit the way they always have since I've been home with my back. \n\nI know some people probably drop a lot of weight when they... you know, do what I've been doing, but I just haven't. I almost feel a little silly saying it out loud, going through all that and not even... well, not that I *should* be doing it, of course. I know it's a terrible habit and I really do want to stop, I promise I do. \n\nI hope I'm not confusing things? I just haven't really noticed any big drop on the scale at all. Um... well, I wouldn't say I think I look abnormal, exactly. I mean, I know I'm not, like, huge or anything. But... I don't know, sometimes it's just really hard to see myself the way other people do. 

Like, if I go to put on my work pants and they feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm just walking by the mirror and I catch a glimpse of my belly... I just feel awful. It's like, in that moment, I feel like I'm just failing at everything. I used to be this strong woman, you know? I could handle heavy transmissions and keep right up with all the guys at the shop. And now... I just feel like I'm losing that part of myself. 

I know it probably sounds like I'm being too hard on myself, and I really don't mean to sound like I'm complaining. I just... I want to feel like me again. I hope you don't think I'm being too dramatic about it? I just really want to stay strong.

Even if someone said I looked fine, I'd probably still be looking in the mirror and seeing all the places where things just feel too soft, especially since I haven't been able to work at the shop and move around like I used to. \n\nBut I know I shouldn't think that way if people tell me otherwise. I really do try to trust what others see. I hope that makes sense? I don't want to sound like I'm completely ignoring reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, my weight has stayed pretty much exactly the same, give or take a pound or two. My clothes still fit the way they always have since I've been home with my back. \n\nI know some people probably drop a lot of weight when they... you know, do what I've been doing, but I just haven't. I almost feel a little silly saying it out loud, going through all that and not even... well, not that I *should* be doing it, of course. Um... yeah, I guess it is the main reason. I just... I get so anxious about it. I really hope you don't think I'm completely obsessed or anything, but... 

It's just that if I step on the bathroom scale and I see it's even a pound up, I just... I panic. It makes me feel like all that time I spent laid up with my bad back is just turning me soft, you know? Like I'm losing all the muscle I built up working on cars. And before I can even think straight, I'm already planning how to... well, how to undo it. 

I know it's not a healthy way to handle it, and I really am trying to be better about it. I just want to stay strong and capable. I hope you can understand why it scares me so much? I'm not trying to be difficult, I promise.

Um... well, it's... I guess it's a bit of both, but... maybe the control part is a bigger piece of it than I want to admit. I really hate saying this out loud because I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart, but...

I don't want to be unreasonable. \n\nIt's just that... well, I guess I'd still feel... heavy? Or maybe just not right in my own skin. Even if someone said I looked fine, I'd probably still be looking in the mirror and seeing all the places where things just feel too soft, especially since I haven't been able to work at the shop and move around like I used to. \n\nBut I know I shouldn't think that way if people tell me otherwise. I really do try to trust what others see. I hope that makes sense? It's just... well, since I've been stuck at home with this back injury, not working at the shop or moving around the way I used to, I just feel like I'm turning soft. \n\nEven if the scale just goes up a single pound, I... I kind of panic a little bit. I know it's probably silly to get worked up over one pound, but my mind just starts racing, trying to figure out how to undo it before I can even think straight. I don't want you to think I'm obsessed or anything, I just... I really want to make sure I don't let myself go while I'm laid up. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up and doing that all the time? I really am trying my best to manage it normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you lost a lot of weight, but then gained it back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I haven't ever really had a time where I dropped a whole bunch of weight and then put it all back on. \n\nI hope that's okay? Even before I hurt my back and had to stop working at the shop, it was always just about the same. I try really hard to keep it that way, you know? I don't want to be one of those people whose weight is all over the place. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is abnormal even when others tell you it's normal?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think I look abnormal, exactly. I could handle heavy transmissions and keep right up with all the guys at the shop. And now... I just feel like I'm losing that part of myself. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm being too hard on myself, and I really don't mean to sound like I'm complaining. I just... But I do try other things, I promise. I try to skip meals when I can, or just have a little bit. I know throwing up isn't the right way to handle it, and I really am trying to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up and doing that all the time? I really am trying my best to manage it normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you lost a lot of weight, but then gained it back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my weight has pretty much always just stayed right around where it is now. I haven't ever really had a time where I dropped a whole bunch of weight and then put it all back on. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know some people's weight goes up and down a lot, like a yo-yo, but mine has just been... steady, I guess. Even before I hurt my back and had to stop working at the shop, it was always just about the same. I try really hard to keep it that way, you know? I don't want to be one of those people whose weight is all over the place. I just feel like I'm losing that part of myself. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm being too hard on myself, and I really don't mean to sound like I'm complaining. I just... I mean, I really try to just eat less or, you know, be careful about what I have. I don't want you to think I'm completely out of control or that I rely on... on throwing up for everything. \n\nIt's just that sometimes, when I've had too much, it feels like the quickest way to fix it before things get worse. But I do try other things, I promise. I try to skip meals when I can, or just have a little bit. I know throwing up isn't the right way to handle it, and I really am trying to be better about it. I hope you don't think I'm just giving up and doing that all the time? I really am trying my best to manage it normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you lost a lot of weight, but then gained it back?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my weight has pretty much always just stayed right around where it is now. I haven't ever really had a time where I dropped a whole bunch of weight and then put it all back on. \n\nI hope that's okay? I know some people's weight goes up and down a lot, like a yo-yo, but mine has just been... steady, I guess. Even before I hurt my back and had to stop working at the shop, it was always just about the same. I try really hard to keep it that way, you know? I don't want to be one of those people whose weight is all over the place. I hope that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is abnormal even when others tell you it's normal?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say I think I look abnormal, exactly. I mean, I know I'm not, like, huge or anything. But... I don't know, sometimes it's just really hard to see myself the way other people do. \n\nLike, if I go to put on my work pants and they feel even a little bit tight, or if I'm just walking by the mirror and I catch a glimpse of my belly... I just feel awful. It's like, in that moment, I feel like I'm just failing at everything. I used to be this strong woman, you know? I could handle heavy transmissions and keep right up with all the guys at the shop. And now... I just feel like I'm losing that part of myself. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm being too hard on myself, and I really don't mean to sound like I'm complaining. I just... I want to feel like me again. I hope you don't think I'm being too dramatic about it? I just really want to stay strong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there are specific parts of your body that are unacceptably fat or unattractive, even if other people tell you they look fine?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I'm really trying to keep a good perspective on things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're throwing up after eating because you're afraid of gaining weight or because you feel like you've lost control of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of... it's a little bit of both, I guess. I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. \n\nI know it sounds awful, and I'm really ashamed to even admit it. I just... I get so terrified of gaining weight, especially since I'm not moving around at the shop like I used to. I just want to keep things balanced, you know? But lately, it's gotten to the point where... I'm a little scared I can't stop doing it. I really didn't want to tell anyone because I don't want you to think I'm crazy or that I'm not trying my best. I am trying, I promise. \n\nI hope you're not too disappointed in me for doing that? I just... I want to fix it, and I want to do the right thing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it is the main reason. I just... I try to keep it under control, but... it's gotten to the point where I'm actually scared I can't stop doing it. \n\nI know it probably sounds terrible, and I'm sorry to just lay that on you right away. Is that... does that make sense? I just hope I'm in the right place.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try to keep it to a minimum, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm just doing it all the time. But... if I look at, say, the past three months? There's probably hardly been a week where I didn't have one of those late-night eating episodes and then... well, go make myself throw up afterward. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds bad. Some weeks it does happen several times... especially when I'm home more because of my back. I try to be good, I really do, but when I'm stuck at home resting it just gets harder to avoid. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm completely out of control. It makes me feel like all this time I've spent laid up with my back is just... turning me soft, you know? And I'll just start planning how to undo it before I can even think straight. \n\nI know it sounds so silly to get worked up over one pound. I really should know better at my age, and I am trying to be more reasonable about it. Does... does that sound totally crazy to you? I hope you don't think I'm just being shallow.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or types of food you eat to try to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... actually, no, I don't really do that. I don't really cut out certain foods or put myself on strict diets or anything like that. \n\nI know a lot of women do that to stay in shape, and maybe I should have better willpower about my meals, but... I just try to eat normal things. I've never wanted to be one of those difficult people who can't eat anything when they go out or who makes a big fuss over a menu. I hope that's the right way to go about it... is it? \n\nIt's really just those times when I go overboard and eat too much at once that are the problem. I try to be good the rest of the time without starving myself. I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be fat even if other people told you that you are underweight or at a normal weight?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I wouldn't say I'd think I was *fat*, exactly. I mean, if a doctor or someone told me I was actually underweight, I'd want to believe them, I really would. I don't want to be unreasonable. \n\nIt's just that... well, I guess I'd still feel... heavy? Or maybe just not right in my own skin. Even if someone said I looked fine, I'd probably still be looking in the mirror and seeing all the places where things just feel too soft, especially since I haven't been able to work at the shop and move around like I used to. \n\nBut I know I shouldn't think that way if people tell me otherwise. I really do try to trust what others see. I hope that makes sense? I don't want to sound like I'm completely ignoring reality or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since you started making yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I hope it's okay to admit that. I mean, my weight has stayed pretty much exactly the same, give or take a pound or two. My clothes still fit the way they always have since I've been home with my back. \n\nI know some people probably drop a lot of weight when they... you know, do what I've been doing, but I just haven't. I don't want to be one of those people whose weight is all over the place. Um... gosh, that's another really hard one. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm struggling to give you a straight answer today. 

But... I guess if I hadn't eaten too much... if I just had a normal amount, like a regular person... I don't think I would feel that same intense panic to get rid of it. It's really when I lose control and eat way more than I meant to that the urge just takes over. 

If I just had a small salad or something, I think I could handle keeping it down. I mean, I hope I could. Does that make sense? I'm sorry if I'm making this complicated, I just want to make sure I'm explaining it the right way so you can help me.

I guess it's both, really. I mean, I do it because I'm just so terrified of the weight gain, like I mentioned. I just want to empty it all out and make the feeling go away. \n\nI know it sounds awful, and I'm really sorry if I'm not making perfect sense. I just...
